TITLE I—COMMODITIES  7
Subtitle A—Commodity Policy  8
SEC. 1101. SUSPENSION OF PERMANENT PRICE SUPPORT  9
AUTHORITY. 10
Section 1602 of the Agricultural Act of 2014 (7  11
U.S.C. 9092) is amended by striking ‘‘2023’’ each place  12
it appears and inserting ‘‘2031’’. 13
SEC. 1102. SERVICING OF LOANS.  14
(a) M ARKETING ASSISTANCE LOANS.—Section 1204  15
of the Agricultural Act of 2014 (7 U.S.C. 9034) is amend -16
ed by adding at the end the following: 17
‘‘(j) E FFECT OF LAPSE IN APPROPRIATIONS.—The  18

‘‘SEC. 1267A. ESTABLISHMENT AND PURPOSES. 19
‘‘(a) E STABLISHMENT.—The Secretary shall estab -20
lish a program, to be known as the ‘Forest Conservation  21
Easement Program’, for the conservation and restoration  22
of eligible land and natural resources through the acquisi -23
tion of forest land easements or forest reserve easements.  24
115 
RYA26731 1TW [file 1 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(b) P URPOSES.—The purposes of the Program  1
are—  2
‘‘(1) to protect the viability and sustainability  3
of working forests and related conservation values of  4
eligible land and limit the negative effects of non -5
forest uses; 6
‘‘(2) to protect and enhance forest ecosystem  7
and landscape functions and values;  8
‘‘(3) to promote the restoration, protection, and  9
improvement of habitats of species that are threat -10
ened, endangered, or otherwise at-risk; and  11
‘‘(4) to carry out the purposes and functions of  12
the healthy forests reserve program established  13
under title V of the Healthy Forests Restoration Act  14
of 2003 (16 U.S.C. 6571 et seq.) (as in effect on the  15
day before the date of enactment of this section).  16

‘‘(2) C RITERIA.—The Secretary shall establish  10

‘‘(3) PRIORITY.—In evaluating offers under this  13
subsection, the Secretary shall give priority to an  14

greatest conservation benefit—  16
‘‘(A) primarily, to a species listed as en -17

1533); and 20
‘‘(B) secondarily, to any species that—  21
‘‘(i) is not listed as endangered or  22
threatened under that section; but  23
128 
RYA26731 1TW [file 1 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(ii)(I) is a candidate for that listing  1
or listing as a State-listed species or spe -2
cies of special concern; or  3
‘‘(II) is designated by a State wildlife  4
action plan as a species of greatest con -5
servation need. 6
‘‘(4) O THER CONSIDERATIONS .—The Secretary  7
may give additional consideration to eligible land the  8
enrollment under this section of which will—  9
‘‘(A) improve biological diversity;  10
‘‘(B) restore a native forest ecosystem;  11
‘‘(C) conserve forest land that provides a  12
habitat for any species described in paragraph  13
(3); 14
‘‘(D) reduce fragmentation of forest land;  15
or 16
‘‘(E) increase carbon sequestration.  17
‘‘(5) T REATMENT.—The Secretary may evalu -18

by landowners who are socially disadvantaged forest  20
landowners separately from offers submitted by  21
other landowners. 22

SEC. 2801. WATERSHED PROTECTION AND FLOOD PREVEN -8
TION ACT. 9
(a) SHORT TITLE; FINDINGS; DECLARATION OF POL-10
ICY.—The first section of the Watershed Protection and  11
Flood Prevention Act (16 U.S.C. 1001) is amended to  12
read as follows: 13
‘‘SECTION 1. SHORT TITLE; FINDINGS; DECLARATION OF  14
POLICY. 15
‘‘(a) S HORT TITLE.—This Act may be cited as the  16
‘Watershed Protection and Flood Prevention Act’. 17
‘‘(b) F INDINGS.—Congress finds that erosion, flood -18
water, drought, and sediment damages in the watersheds  19
of the rivers and streams of the United States cause loss  20
of life, declines in agricultural production, harm to fish,  21
birds, wildlife, and recreation opportunities, and damage  22
to property, thereby constituting a menace to the national  23
welfare. 24
149 
RYA26731 1TW [file 1 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(c) S ENSE OF CONGRESS.—It is the sense of Con -1
gress that the Federal Government should cooperate with  2
local organizations for the purposes of—  3
‘‘(1) preventing the damages described in sub -4
section (b); 5
‘‘(2) furthering the conservation, management,  6
development, utilization, and disposal of water, and  7
the conservation and utilization of land; and  8
‘‘(3) thereby preserving, protecting, and improv -9
ing land and water resources and the quality of the  10
environment.’’. 11
(b) D EFINITIONS.—Section 2 of the Watershed Pro -12
tection and Flood Prevention Act (16 U.S.C. 1002) is  13
amended to read as follows: 14

SEC. 2803. FERAL SWINE ERADICATION AND CONTROL PRO -3
GRAM. 4
(a) I N GENERAL.—Chapter 5 of subtitle D of title  5

‘‘SEC. 1240N. FERAL SWINE ERADICATION AND CONTROL  9
PROGRAM. 10
‘‘(a) I N GENERAL.—The Secretary shall establish a  11
feral swine eradication and control program (referred to  12
in this section as the ‘program’) to respond to the threat  13
feral swine pose to agriculture, native ecosystems, and  14
human and animal health. 15
‘‘(b) D UTIES OF THE SECRETARY.—In carrying out  16
the program, the Secretary shall—  17
‘‘(1) study and assess the nature and extent of  18
damage caused by feral swine to the areas described  19
in subsection (e);  20
‘‘(2) develop methods to eradicate or control  21
feral swine in those areas;  22
‘‘(3) develop methods to restore damage caused  23
by feral swine; and  24
176 
RYA26731 1TW [file 1 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(4) provide financial assistance to agricultural  1
producers in those areas.  2
‘‘(c) ASSISTANCE.—The Secretary may provide finan -3
cial assistance to agricultural producers under the pro -4
gram to implement methods—  5
‘‘(1) to eradicate or control feral swine in the  6
areas described in subsection (e); and  7
‘‘(2) to restore damage caused by feral swine.  8
‘‘(d) C OORDINATION.—The Secretary shall ensure  9
that the Natural Resources Conservation Service and the  10
Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service coordinate for 11
purposes of this section through State technical commit -12
tees established under section 1261(a). 13
‘‘(e) AREAS.—The Secretary shall carry out the pro -14
gram in areas of States in which feral swine have been  15
identified as a threat to agriculture, native ecosystems, or  16
human or animal health, as determined by the Secretary.  17
‘‘(f) COST SHARING.—  18
‘‘(1) F EDERAL SHARE .—The Federal share of  19
the costs of activities under the program may not  20

‘‘(2) I N-KIND CONTRIBUTIONS .—The non-Fed -23
eral share of the costs of activities under the pro -24

(b) P URPOSES.—The purposes of this section are—  1
(1) to create more resilient local and regional  2
food systems; 3
(2) to expand, diversify, and increase resilience  4
in meat and poultry processing and rendering activi -5
ties; 6
(3) to increase farmer and rancher access to  7
animal slaughter options;  8
(4) to improve compliance of processors with  9
livestock and poultry processing statutes (including  10
regulations), including the Federal Meat Inspection  11
Act (21 U.S.C. 601 et seq.) and the Poultry Prod -12
ucts Inspection Act (21 U.S.C. 451 et seq.);  13
(5) to reduce barriers to entry for new meat  14
and poultry processors and renderers;  15
(6) to establish new, or update, expand, or oth -16
erwise improve existing, meat and poultry processing  17
and rendering facilities; and  18
(7) to support the processing and slaughtering  19
of niche production methods such as halal, kosher,  20
and other specific cultural methods.  21

SEC. 8218. CATEGORICAL EXCLUSION FOR HIGH-PRIORITY  15
HAZARD TREES. 16
(a) DEFINITIONS.—In this section:  17
(1) H IGH-PRIORITY HAZARD TREE .—The term  18
‘‘high-priority hazard tree’’ means a standing tree  19
that—  20
(A) presents a visible hazard to people or  21
property due to conditions such as deterioration  22
of, or damage to, the root system, trunk, stem,  23
or limbs of the tree, or the direction or lean of  24
the tree, as determined by the responsible offi -25
cial; 26
555 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(B) is determined by the responsible offi -1
cial to be highly likely to fail and, on failure,  2
would be highly likely to cause injury to people  3
or damage to Federal property; and  4
(C) is located within 300 feet of—  5
(i) a National Forest System road  6
with a maintenance level of 3, 4, or 5;  7
(ii) a National Forest System trail;  8
(iii) a developed recreation site or an  9
administrative facility that is operated and  10
maintained by the Secretary on National  11
Forest System land; or  12
(iv) a project work site.  13
(2) H IGH-PRIORITY HAZARD TREE ACTIVITY .—  14
(A) I N GENERAL .—The term ‘‘high-pri -15
ority hazard tree activity’’ means a forest man -16
agement activity that mitigates the risks associ -17
ated with high-priority hazard trees, including  18
pruning, felling, blasting, cabling, bracing, and  19
disposal of a high-priority hazard tree.  20
(B) E XCLUSIONS.—The term ‘‘high-pri -21
ority hazard tree activity’’ does not include any  22
activity—  23
(i) conducted in a wilderness area or  24
wilderness study area;  25
556 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(ii) for the construction of a perma -1
nent road or permanent trail;  2
(iii) conducted on Federal land on  3
which, by Act of Congress or Presidential  4
proclamation, the removal of vegetation is  5
restricted or prohibited;  6
(iv) conducted in an area in which ac -7
tivities described in subparagraph (A)  8
would be inconsistent with the applicable  9
land and resource management plan; or  10
(v) conducted in an inventoried  11
roadless area. 12
(b) CATEGORICAL EXCLUSION.—  13
(1) I N GENERAL .—High-priority hazard tree  14
activities are a category of actions considered an ac -15
tion categorically excluded from the requirements of  16
Public Law 91–190 (42 U.S.C. 4321 et seq.).  17
(2) P ROJECT SIZE LIMITATION .—A project car -18
ried out using the categorical exclusion established  19
by paragraph (1) may not exceed 6,000 acres.  20

SEC. 8411. BENTON MACKAYE NATIONAL SCENIC TRAIL  22
FEASIBILITY STUDY. 23
(a) F INDINGS.—Congress finds that the Benton  24
MacKaye Trail—  25
646 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(1) is a scenic, nonmotorized trail that traverses  1
approximately 287 miles over some of the most re -2
nowned forests and mountains in the States of Geor -3
gia, Tennessee, and North Carolina;  4
(2) showcases a wide variety of scenic views, in -5
cluding high-elevation peaks, mature forests, and se -6
cluded waterfalls; 7
(3) passes through wilderness that is rich with  8
biodiversity; 9
(4) passes through six designated Wilderness  10
Areas, the Great Smoky Mountains National Park,  11
and the Chattahoochee-Oconee, Cherokee, and  12
Nantahala National Forests;  13
(5) provides a wide array of easily accessible  14
hikes for novice and intermediate hikers, as well as  15
more challenging trails for backpacking;  16
(6) supports economic opportunities for numer -17
ous rural communities which benefit from the visi -18
tors of the trail spending their money on accom -19
modations, food, and outdoor supplies;  20
(7) runs from Springer Mountain in Georgia,  21
through Tennessee, and extends through the Great  22
Smoky Mountains National Park in North Carolina;  23
(8) is physically accessible; and  24
647 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(9) can be maintained at a minimal cost be -1
cause—  2
(A) the Benton MacKaye Trail has been  3
maintained at its current length since 2005;  4
(B) 95 percent of the Benton MacKaye  5
Trail is located on Federal land; and  6
(C) the Benton MacKaye Trail has been  7
successfully constructed, maintained, and man -8
aged by the Benton MacKaye Trail Association  9
since 1980. 10

SEC. 8501. WHITE OAK RESTORATION INITIATIVE COALI -10
TION. 11

(1) as a voluntary collaborative group of Fed -15

in subsection (b); and  19

‘‘White Oak Initiative Coalition Charter’’ adopted by  21
the White Oak Initiative Board of Directors on  22

the charter referred to in subsection (a)(2), the duties of  25
the Coalition are—  26
651 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(1) to coordinate Federal, State, Tribal, local,  1
private, and nongovernmental activities for the res -2
toration of white oak trees and forests that support  3
white oak trees in the United States; and  4
(2) to make program and policy recommenda -5
tions, consistent with applicable forest management  6
plans, with respect to—  7
(A) changes necessary to address Federal  8
and State policies that impede activities to im -9
prove the health, resiliency, and natural regen -10
eration of white oak trees;  11
(B) adopting or modifying Federal and  12
State policies to increase the pace and scale of  13
white oak regeneration and the resiliency of  14
white oak trees;  15
(C) options to enhance communication, co -16
ordination, and collaboration among forest land  17
owners, particularly with respect to cross-  18
boundary projects, to improve the health, resil -19
iency, and natural regeneration of white oak  20
trees; 21
(D) research gaps that should be ad -22
dressed to improve the best available science on  23
white oaks; 24
652 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(E) outreach to forest landowners the land  1
of which possesses white oak trees or white oak  2
regeneration potential, as determined by the  3
Coalition; and 4
(F) options and policies necessary to im -5
prove the quality and quantity of white oak  6

SUPPORT.—The Secretaries shall make available to the  9

SEC. 8503. WHITE OAK REGENERATION AND UPLAND OAK  13
HABITAT. 14
(a) ESTABLISHMENT.—Not later than 180 days after  15
the date of enactment of this Act, the Secretary shall es -16
tablish a nonregulatory program, to be known as the  17
‘‘White Oak and Upland Oak Habitat Regeneration Pro -18
gram’’ (referred to in this section as the ‘‘Program’’). 19
(b) P URPOSES.—The purposes of the Program shall  20
include—  21
(1) coordinating restoration and conservation  22
activities among Federal, State, and local entities,  23
Indian Tribes, and conservation partners to address  24
white oak restoration priorities;  25
654 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(2) improving and regenerating white oak and  1
upland oak forests and the wildlife habitat such for -2
ests provide; 3
(3) carrying out coordinated restoration and  4
conservation activities that lead to the increased  5
growth of species of white oak in native white oak  6
regions; 7
(4) facilitating strategic planning to maximize  8
the resilience of white oak systems and habitats  9
under changing climate conditions;  10
(5) engaging the public through outreach, edu -11
cation, and citizen involvement to increase capacity  12
and support for coordinated restoration and con -13
servation activities for species of white oak; and  14
(6) increasing scientific capacity to support the  15
planning, monitoring, and research activities nec -16
essary to carry out such coordinated restoration and  17
conservation activities. 18
(c) C ONSULTATION.—In establishing the Program,  19
the Secretary, acting through the Chief of the Forest Serv -20
ice, shall consult with—  21
(1) the heads of Federal agencies, including—  22
(A) the Director of the United States Fish  23
and Wildlife Service; and  24
655 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(B) the Chief of the Natural Resources  1
Conservation Service; 2
(2) the Governor of each State in which res -3
toration efforts will be carried out pursuant to the  4
Program; and 5
(3) Indian Tribes.  6
(d) D UTIES.—In carrying out the Program, the Sec -7
retary shall—  8
(1) draw on the best available science and man -9
agement plans for species of white oak to identify,  10
prioritize, and implement restoration and conserva -11
tion activities that will improve the growth of white  12
oak trees in the United States;  13
(2) collaborate and coordinate with the White  14
Oak Restoration Initiative Coalition established by  15
section 8501(a) to prioritize white oak restoration  16
initiatives; 17
(3) adopt a white oak restoration strategy  18
that—  19
(A) supports the implementation of a  20
shared set of science-based restoration and con -21
servation activities developed in accordance with  22
paragraph (1); 23
(B) targets cost-effective projects with  24
measurable results; and  25
656 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(C) maximizes restoration outcomes with  1
no net gain of Federal full-time equivalent em -2
ployees; and 3
(4) establish voluntary grant and technical as -4
sistance programs in accordance with subsection (e).  5
(e) GRANTS AND ASSISTANCE.—  6
(1) DEFINITIONS.—In this subsection:  7
(A) F OUNDATION.—The term ‘‘Founda -8

program’’ means the voluntary grant and tech -12

as the ‘‘agencies’’). 19

entered into under subsection (a) shall include—  21

of regional drought conditions;  24
678 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(2) a strategy for amending those determina -1
tions to ensure consistent policy with respect to  2
drought response in cases where the agencies are  3
making inconsistent determinations within the same  4
spatial scale; 5

tainable aviation fuels development, deployment, and  22
commercialization; 23

farmers, ranchers, foresters, and agricultural pro -25

of sustainable aviation fuels.  7

SEC. 10202. SOIL HEALTH STUDY.  11
(a) S TUDY.—The Secretary shall conduct a study to  12
assess the types of, and practices using, plant biostimu -13
lants (as defined in section 2 of the Federal Insecticide,  14
Fungicide, and Rodenticide Act (7 U.S.C. 136)) that best  15
achieve the following: 16
(1) Increasing organic matter content.  17
(2) Reducing atmospheric volatilization.  18
(3) Promotion of nutrient management prac -19
tices. 20
(4) Limiting or eliminating runoff or leaching  21
of soil or nutrients, such as phosphorus and nitro -22
gen, into groundwater or other water sources.  23
(5) Restoring beneficial bioactivity or healthy  24
nutrients to the soil.  25
753 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(6) Aiding in carbon sequestration, nutrient use  1
efficiency, and other climate-related benefits.  2
(7) Supporting innovative approaches to im -3
proving agricultural sustainability, including the  4
adoption of performance-based outcome standards  5
and criteria. 6
(b) R EPORT.—Not later than 4 years after the date  7
on which funds are first made available for the study  8
under subsection (a), the Secretary shall make publicly  9
available and submit to the Committee on Agriculture of  10
the House of Representatives and the Committee on Agri -11

The Federal Crop Insurance Act is amended by in -9

‘‘SEC. 508E. PILOT PROGRAM TO REVIEW EFFECTIVENESS  11

‘‘(a) I N GENERAL.—The Risk Management Agency  13

ferred to in this section as the ‘pilot program’), to be in  15
effect beginning with the 2027 crop year, to evaluate the  16
effectiveness of the reduction in benefits applied to corn  17
and other crops, as determined by the Corporation, plant -18
ed during the late planting period (as defined in section  19
457.8 of title 7, Code of Federal Regulations (or a suc -20
cessor regulation)). 21
‘‘(b) L OCATION.—The pilot program shall be con -22
ducted in not less than 4 counties located within or adja -23
cent to the North Plains Groundwater Conservation Dis -24
787 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
trict or the Panhandle Groundwater Conservation District  1
in the State of Texas. 2
‘‘(c) DURATION.—The pilot program shall operate for  3
a period of not less than 4 crop years. 4
‘‘(d) E VALUATION.—In carrying out the pilot pro -5
gram, the Risk Management Agency and the Corporation  6
shall—  7
‘‘(1) suspend any reduction to the insurance  8
guarantee applied to an insurance policy for a crop  9
that is planted during the late planting period;  10
‘‘(2) gather and analyze data to determine if  11
the number of days beyond the final plant date in  12
which a crop was planted during the late planting  13
period correlates with a decrease in crop yields; and  14
‘‘(3) determine if planting a crop after the final  15
plant date results in reduced usage of irrigation  16
from the Ogallala Aquifer.  17
‘‘(e) P ARTNERSHIPS.—Of the amounts made avail -18
able under section 522(e)(2)(A)(ii), the Corporation may  19
use not more than $200,000 to enter into a partnership  20
or cooperative agreement with a nonprofit organization,  21
State agency, or public university that is familiar with ag -22
ricultural production in the area described in subsection  23
(b) to conduct the research and evaluation required under  24
paragraphs (2) and (3) of subsection (d). 25
788 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(f) REPORT.—Not later than 90 days after the last  1

Committee on Agriculture of the House of Representatives  5
a report that includes—  6
‘‘(1) a summary of the results of the pilot pro -7

‘‘(2) an analysis of the correlation between  9

SEC. 12106. ALLOWED INTEREST OF LIVESTOCK AUCTION  10
OWNERS IN MEATPACKING BUSINESSES. 11
(a) I N GENERAL.—Not later than 1 year after the  12
date of enactment of this Act, the Secretary shall revise  13
section 201.67 of title 9, Code of Federal Regulations (as  14
in effect on the date of enactment of this Act), to exempt  15
the packers described in subsection (b) from the prohibi -16
tion described in that section. 17
(b) P ACKERS DESCRIBED.—A packer referred to in  18
subsection (a) is a packer that—  19
(1) with respect to cattle or sheep, has a cumu -20
lative slaughter capacity of less than—  21
(A) 2,000 animals per day; or  22
(B) 700,000 animals per year; and  23
(2) with respect to hogs, has a cumulative  24
slaughter capacity of less than—  25
818 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
(A) 10,000 animals per day; or  1
(B) 3,000,000 animals per year.  2
(c) R EQUIRED DISCLOSURE.—In the case of a mar -3
ket agency that has an ownership interest in, finances, or  4
participates in the management or operation of a packer  5
to which the market agency sells consigned livestock, the  6
market agency shall disclose on the account of sale the  7
name of the packer and the nature of the relationship be -8
tween the market agency and the packer. 9
(d) S AVINGS CLAUSE.—Nothing in this section shall  10
limit the authority of the Secretary to adopt or enforce  11
rules or regulations under the Packers and Stockyards  12
Act, 1921 (7 U.S.C. 181 et seq.), relating to the protection  13
of producers, competition, market integrity, or the preven -14
tion of conflicts of interest. 15

closure Act of 1978 (as redesignated by section  3
12201(b)(1)(A)) is amended to read as follows: 4
‘‘SEC. 4. CIVIL PENALTIES. 5
‘‘(a) ASSESSMENT.—  6
‘‘(1) I N GENERAL .—The Secretary shall assess  7
against a person a civil penalty if the Secretary de -8
termines that the person has committed a violation  9
of this Act by—  10
‘‘(A) failing to submit a report required  11
under section 3 in accordance with that section;  12
‘‘(B) knowingly submitting a report under  13
section 3 that—  14
‘‘(i) does not contain all information  15
required to appear in the report; or  16
‘‘(ii) contains information that is mis -17
leading or false; or  18
‘‘(C) failing to update any report sub -19
mitted under section 3 that presents informa -20
tion that is no longer accurate.  21
‘‘(2) A MOUNT.—The amount of a civil penalty  22
assessed under paragraph (1) shall be such amount  23
as the Secretary determines to be appropriate to  24
achieve the purposes of this Act, subject to the con -25
ditions that—  26
844 
RYA26670 NDT [file 2 of 2]  S.L.C. 
‘‘(A) the amount determined for a violation  1
described in subparagraph (A) or (C) of para -2
graph (1) shall not exceed 25 percent of the  3
fair market value, on the date of the assess -4
ment, of the interest in agricultural land with  5
respect to which the applicable violation oc -6
curred; and 7
‘‘(B) the amount determined for a violation  8
described in paragraph (1)(B) shall be not less  9
than 5 percent, but not more than 25 percent,  10
of the fair market value, on the date of the as -11
sessment, of the interest in agricultural land  12
with respect to which the applicable violation  13
occurred. 14
‘‘(b) C OLLECTION.—A civil penalty assessed under  15
subsection (a) shall be recoverable in a civil action brought  16
by the Attorney General of the United States in a United  17
States district court of competent jurisdiction. 18
‘‘(c) A VAILABILITY.—A civil penalty collected under  19
this section shall be available to the Secretary, and remain  20
available until expended, for the purpose of enforcing this  21
Act. 22
‘‘(d) LIENS.—  23

under subsection (a), the Secretary may place a lien  25
845
